IBD fecal metagenomic with calprotectin

q01-ibd-calprotectin

Gold SQL
-- version: 1 (SUPERSEDED by version 2 below -- kept for history, not executed)
-- date: 2026-10-05
-- reason: initial authoring. Seeded from the translator's 2026-10-05_initial run (which
--   recognized "IBD diagnosis" and "calprotectin measurement" as jattr concepts but then
--   also wrote an overly broad native-SQL bucket that swamped them via UNION semantics --
--   see notes.md). Independently verified against real mirror values: the jattr key
--   fecalcalprotectin_run exists on exactly 134 rows mirror-wide, ALL of them in bioproject
--   PRJNA1265906 -- confirmed via two independent counts that agree (134 == 134).
--   Fecal calprotectin is a standard clinical biomarker used almost exclusively to monitor
--   IBD (Crohn's disease / ulcerative colitis) activity, so a dataset recording it is IBD
--   research by biological convention even though no literal "IBD" text appears anywhere
--   in this mirror's study/sample_name/attributes for this bioproject (confirmed by a prior
--   session, 2026-08-25).
--
-- SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
-- WHERE bioproject = 'PRJNA1265906'
--   AND json_extract_string(jattr, '$.fecalcalprotectin_run') IS NOT NULL;

-- version: 2 (current)
-- date: 2026-10-08
-- reason: v1's "calprotectin implies IBD by clinical convention" assumption was explicitly
--   rejected by the researcher (2026-10-08) -- gold requires an EXPLICIT IBD-type jattr key
--   on the SAME sample as an explicit calprotectin-type jattr key, not an inferred link.
--   Re-derived independently (not via the translator/pipeline under test): any of the real
--   IBD-naming variants AND any of the real calprotectin-naming variants (found via the
--   rebuilt attribute_keys_index) must both be present, non-null, on one biosample.
--   Result: 665 biosamples, ALL in bioproject PRJEB18471 -- a DIFFERENT bioproject than v1's
--   PRJNA1265906, which has no explicit IBD field at all and is correctly excluded under v2.
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE jattr IS NOT NULL
  AND list_filter(json_extract_string(jattr, [
        '$.ibd_diagnosis_sam', '$.ibd_disease_sam', '$.ibd_sam', '$.ibd_diagnosis_refined_sam',
        '$.ibd_or_not_sam', '$.ibd_type_sam', '$.ibd_subtype_sam', '$.ibd_history_sam',
        '$.inflam_bowel_disease_sam', '$.inflammatory_bowel_disease_sam',
        '$.ibd___0_no__1_yes_sam', '$.ibd_case_control_sam', '$.crohns_disease_sam', '$.ibdstatus_sam'
      ]), x -> x IS NOT NULL) != []
  AND list_filter(json_extract_string(jattr, [
        '$.calprotectin_sam', '$.calprotectin_numeric_sam', '$.calprotectin0_sam',
        '$.calprotectin_ms_sam', '$.value_calprotectin_sam', '$.calprotectin_units_sam',
        '$.feacal_calprotectin_sam', '$.fecal_calprotectin_sam', '$.fecalcalprotectin_run',
        '$.host_fecal_calprotectin_mgpergdryweight_sam', '$.baseline_calprotectin_sam'
      ]), x -> x IS NOT NULL) != [];
MATCH 2026-10-19T00:00:00 · code_change · gold v2

Supersedes the PARTIAL judgment from the same run_id: a value-aware check found 81.8% (979/1197) have an affirmative value on both matched concepts; the remaining 18.2% have a negative/missing value (e.g. ibd_sam=False, calprotectin=NA/not collected) on at least one side. Researcher confirmed presence/content matching is deliberately value-agnostic by design -- a recorded False/NA value still means the field exists, which is what concept search is meant to surface. The low raw precision against gold v2 (55.6%) was gold v2 incompleteness (missed real calprotectin key variants; could not practically hand-enumerate all ~27 generically-named keys holding IBD-type values), not a system defect. See notes.md addendum.

plan: /private/pinto-lab/plans/20261008_123437_ibd-fecal-metagenomic-with-calprotectin/plan.json

Notes
# Run: 2026-10-08_after-distance-threshold-fix

**Context:** recheck against a mirror where all three fixes are live: PR #59 (intersect
semantics), PR #61 (deterministic key eligibility), and PR #62 (distance-threshold +
capped-candidate retrieval, replacing fixed `TOP_K=5`).

## Translation

```json
{
  "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' OR organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%feces metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%metagenome%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%human-gut%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%metagenome%'",
  "jattr_criteria": ["IBD diagnosis", "calprotectin measurement"],
  "jattr_combinator": "all",
  "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"
}
```

## PR #62 confirmed working

"calprotectin measurement" now matches 77 keys/values, **including `fecalcalprotectin_run`**
(the key PR #61's rebuild restored, that the old `TOP_K=5` still couldn't surface). The
distance-threshold retrieval fix is directly verified: the correct key is now actually used.

## But: gold v1 was wrong, not the pipeline

Generated cohort: 1,197 biosamples. Overlap with **gold v1** (134, `PRJNA1265906`): 0. Dug in:
none of the 52,980 biosamples matched by "IBD diagnosis" overlap `PRJNA1265906` at all -- that
bioproject genuinely has no explicit IBD-type jattr field, confirmed directly against the
real mirror. Gold v1's premise ("calprotectin implies IBD by clinical convention, even with
no literal IBD field") was put to the researcher and explicitly **rejected** 2026-10-08: gold
must require an explicit IBD-type field, not an inferred one.

Re-derived gold independently under that stricter rule (see `gold.sql` v2): biosamples with
BOTH an explicit IBD-type jattr key AND an explicit calprotectin-type jattr key, non-null, on
the same sample. Result: **665 biosamples, all in bioproject PRJEB18471** -- a different
bioproject entirely from v1's guess.

## Verification against gold v2

- Overlap: **665 / 665** -- full recall, zero missed.
- Generated-only (extra): 532 (precision 665/1197 = 55.6%).

## Judgment: PARTIAL

100% recall, 55.6% precision. The extra 532 rows are the native-SQL bucket's breadth
(`librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' OR organism ILIKE ...`, a wide net) intersected with
jattr matches that are real but not as tightly scoped as gold's two-key-co-occurrence
rule -- e.g. a sample might match "IBD diagnosis" via a loosely-related value and
"calprotectin measurement" via a different real calprotectin key than gold's own list,
both genuinely jattr-relevant but not meeting gold v2's stricter definition.

This is a precision question, not a correctness bug: the translator's native-SQL bucket is
intentionally broad (any metagenomic/gut sample) and intersected with two independently
resolved jattr concepts -- reasonable behavior. Whether gold v2's narrow co-occurrence
rule or the system's broader jattr-concept-based reach is the "right" granularity for this
kind of question is itself a judgment call, flagged below.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Is 55.6% precision (665/1197) an acceptable outcome for this kind of broad jattr-criteria
question, or should gold v2's narrow co-occurrence definition be treated as the target
precision bar going forward?

## Addendum (2026-10-19): value-polarity is by design, not a defect

A value-aware check across the full 1,197-row cohort found 81.8% (979/1197) have an
affirmative value on both matched concepts, 18.2% have a negative/missing value
(e.g. `ibd_sam='False'`, `calprotectin_ave_sam='NA'`, `'not collected'`) on at least
one side. Researcher confirmed (2026-10-19): **this is intended behavior, not a bug**
-- presence/content matching is deliberately value-agnostic (a sample where
`ibd_sam=False` is recorded still "has an IBD-type field," which is what the concept
search is designed to surface). No resolver change needed.

Net judgment, superseding the PARTIAL call above: the pipeline (PR #59 + #61 + #62)
is working as designed. The low raw precision against gold v2 (55.6%) was an artifact
of gold v2's own incompleteness (missed real calprotectin key variants, and couldn't
practically enumerate all ~27 generically-named keys holding IBD-type values by hand)
-- not a system defect. No further action on this question.
PARTIAL 2026-10-08T12:34:37 · code_change · gold v2

PR #62 (distance threshold) confirmed working: fecalcalprotectin_run now matched for "calprotectin measurement" (was silently dropped before). Gold v1 was wrong, not the pipeline -- its calprotectin-implies-IBD assumption was explicitly rejected by the researcher 2026-10-08. Re-derived gold v2 requires explicit IBD-key + calprotectin-key co-occurrence on the same sample: 665 biosamples, all in PRJEB18471 (different bioproject than v1 guessed). Overlap with gold v2: 665/665 (100% recall), precision 665/1197=55.6%. See notes.md.

❓ Needs input: Is 55.6% precision (665/1197) acceptable for this kind of broad jattr-criteria question, or should gold v2's narrow co-occurrence definition be the precision bar going forward?

Notes
# Run: 2026-10-08_after-distance-threshold-fix

**Context:** recheck against a mirror where all three fixes are live: PR #59 (intersect
semantics), PR #61 (deterministic key eligibility), and PR #62 (distance-threshold +
capped-candidate retrieval, replacing fixed `TOP_K=5`).

## Translation

```json
{
  "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' OR organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%feces metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%metagenome%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%human-gut%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%metagenome%'",
  "jattr_criteria": ["IBD diagnosis", "calprotectin measurement"],
  "jattr_combinator": "all",
  "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"
}
```

## PR #62 confirmed working

"calprotectin measurement" now matches 77 keys/values, **including `fecalcalprotectin_run`**
(the key PR #61's rebuild restored, that the old `TOP_K=5` still couldn't surface). The
distance-threshold retrieval fix is directly verified: the correct key is now actually used.

## But: gold v1 was wrong, not the pipeline

Generated cohort: 1,197 biosamples. Overlap with **gold v1** (134, `PRJNA1265906`): 0. Dug in:
none of the 52,980 biosamples matched by "IBD diagnosis" overlap `PRJNA1265906` at all -- that
bioproject genuinely has no explicit IBD-type jattr field, confirmed directly against the
real mirror. Gold v1's premise ("calprotectin implies IBD by clinical convention, even with
no literal IBD field") was put to the researcher and explicitly **rejected** 2026-10-08: gold
must require an explicit IBD-type field, not an inferred one.

Re-derived gold independently under that stricter rule (see `gold.sql` v2): biosamples with
BOTH an explicit IBD-type jattr key AND an explicit calprotectin-type jattr key, non-null, on
the same sample. Result: **665 biosamples, all in bioproject PRJEB18471** -- a different
bioproject entirely from v1's guess.

## Verification against gold v2

- Overlap: **665 / 665** -- full recall, zero missed.
- Generated-only (extra): 532 (precision 665/1197 = 55.6%).

## Judgment: PARTIAL

100% recall, 55.6% precision. The extra 532 rows are the native-SQL bucket's breadth
(`librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' OR organism ILIKE ...`, a wide net) intersected with
jattr matches that are real but not as tightly scoped as gold's two-key-co-occurrence
rule -- e.g. a sample might match "IBD diagnosis" via a loosely-related value and
"calprotectin measurement" via a different real calprotectin key than gold's own list,
both genuinely jattr-relevant but not meeting gold v2's stricter definition.

This is a precision question, not a correctness bug: the translator's native-SQL bucket is
intentionally broad (any metagenomic/gut sample) and intersected with two independently
resolved jattr concepts -- reasonable behavior. Whether gold v2's narrow co-occurrence
rule or the system's broader jattr-concept-based reach is the "right" granularity for this
kind of question is itself a judgment call, flagged below.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Is 55.6% precision (665/1197) an acceptable outcome for this kind of broad jattr-criteria
question, or should gold v2's narrow co-occurrence definition be treated as the target
precision bar going forward?

## Addendum (2026-10-19): value-polarity is by design, not a defect

A value-aware check across the full 1,197-row cohort found 81.8% (979/1197) have an
affirmative value on both matched concepts, 18.2% have a negative/missing value
(e.g. `ibd_sam='False'`, `calprotectin_ave_sam='NA'`, `'not collected'`) on at least
one side. Researcher confirmed (2026-10-19): **this is intended behavior, not a bug**
-- presence/content matching is deliberately value-agnostic (a sample where
`ibd_sam=False` is recorded still "has an IBD-type field," which is what the concept
search is designed to surface). No resolver change needed.

Net judgment, superseding the PARTIAL call above: the pipeline (PR #59 + #61 + #62)
is working as designed. The low raw precision against gold v2 (55.6%) was an artifact
of gold v2's own incompleteness (missed real calprotectin key variants, and couldn't
practically enumerate all ~27 generically-named keys holding IBD-type values by hand)
-- not a system defect. No further action on this question.
MISMATCH 2026-10-08T09:47:40 · mirror_rebuild · gold v1

Translator correct this time (intersect fix holds, no union-swamping). attribute_keys_index rebuild (PR #61) also confirmed correct: fecalcalprotectin_run now present with all 134 biosamples. But jattr concept search still misses it in production: ranks #27/85143 nearest keys for "calprotectin measurement" (distance 0.817), crowded out of TOP_K=5 by ~18 near-duplicate calprotectin_*_sam naming variants. Overlap=0, gold-only(missed)=134, generated-only(extra)=665. See notes.md.

❓ Needs input: What TOP_K (or a dedup/rerank step) reliably surfaces true matches in naming-duplicate-heavy neighborhoods without blowing up matching cost mirror-wide?

plan: /private/pinto-lab/plans/20261008_094740_ibd-fecal-metagenomic-with-calprotectin/plan.json

-- SRA_METADATA
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE sra_metadata.librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND (sra_metadata.organism ILIKE '%fecal metagenome%' OR sra_metadata.organism ILIKE '%human gut metagenome%' OR sra_metadata.organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' OR sra_metadata.organism ILIKE '%feces metagenome%' OR list_contains(sra_metadata.biosamplemodel_sam, 'MIGS/MIMS/MIMARKS.human-gut'));

Notes
# Run: 2026-10-08_after-index-rebuild

**Context:** first recheck against a mirror where BOTH fixes are live:
PR #59 (`sql_jattr_combinator` intersect semantics) and PR #61 (deterministic,
sampling-free key eligibility -- `attribute_keys_index`/`attribute_values_index`
fully rebuilt 2026-10-07/08, job 32916510).

**Also fixed in this run:** `run_question.py`'s `write_sidecars` was re-executing
`query.sql` directly instead of using the resolved `Cohort` (`plan.discovered`),
silently recording the raw native-SQL bucket's row count instead of the real,
jattr-intersected answer. A first attempt at this recheck (discarded, not
recorded in index.csv) produced 6,567,905 rows this way, even though the real
resolved cohort for that plan was 0. Fixed in commit 764cef4 before this run.

## Translation

```json
{
  "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND (organism ILIKE '%fecal metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%human gut metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%feces metagenome%' OR list_contains(biosamplemodel_sam, 'MIGS/MIMS/MIMARKS.human-gut'))",
  "jattr_criteria": ["IBD diagnosis", "calprotectin measurement"],
  "jattr_combinator": "all",
  "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"
}
```

Correct translation this time: native SQL narrows to fecal/gut metagenomes, jattr
criteria narrow further to IBD+calprotectin, combined via intersect. No repeat of
the original union-swamping bug.

## Verification against gold (134 rows, `fecalcalprotectin_run`, exclusive to PRJNA1265906)

- Generated cohort: **665** biosamples.
- Overlap with gold: **0**. Gold-only (missed): 134. Generated-only (extra): 665.

## Root cause: NOT the key-presence bug PR #61 fixed

Confirmed directly against the real `attribute_keys_index`:
- `fecalcalprotectin_run` **is present** in the rebuilt index (it was absent before
  PR #61) and its row carries all 134 correct biosamples.
- Querying the index for "calprotectin measurement" with `limit(50)` instead of the
  production `TOP_K=5`: `fecalcalprotectin_run` ranks **#27** (distance 0.817) among
  85,143 keys -- a real, close semantic match.
- Why it never surfaces in production: `TOP_K=5` (`src/cohortquery/jattr_concepts.py`)
  returns only the 5 nearest keys. Ranks #1-18 are all near-duplicate
  `calprotectin_*_sam`/`calprotectin*_run` naming variants (`calprotectin_sam`,
  `calprotectin_numeric_sam`, `calprotectin0_sam`, ... `fecal_calprotectin_sam`,
  `baseline_calprotectin_sam`), crowding the true answer out of the top 5 entirely.

This is a **distinct, still-open issue**: retrieval breadth (`TOP_K`), not key
eligibility/classification. The data is correct; the search returns too few
candidates for a vocabulary with this much naming duplication.

## Judgment: MISMATCH

Gold not found. Translator behavior is now correct (intersect fix holds); the
index now contains the right key with the right data; the failure is purely in
how many nearest neighbors get surfaced to the resolver.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

What TOP_K (or a smarter dedup/rerank step) would reliably surface true matches
in naming-duplicate-heavy neighborhoods like this one, without blowing up
matching cost across the whole mirror? Flagging to the user rather than
picking a number unilaterally.
MISMATCH 2026-10-06T11:31:17 · code_change · gold v1

sql_jattr_combinator=intersect fix verified working as designed (996410 -> 436 rows). But ZERO overlap with the 134-row gold: the jattr concept search for calprotectin measurement matched 10 jattr keys (2625 biosamples) but NOT fecalcalprotectin_run, the exact key all 134 gold samples use -- an embedding/concept-search gap, pre-existing, unmasked by this fix. See notes.md.

❓ Needs input: (1) worth fixing the embedding gap for fecalcalprotectin_run? (2) is jattr_combinator=all right for IBD+calprotectin given IBD context may be study-level/implicit rather than per-sample?

plan: /private/pinto-lab/plans/20261006_113117_ibd-fecal-metagenomic-with-calprotectin/plan.json

# q01-ibd-calprotectin — recheck after sql_jattr_combinator fix

**Trigger:** code_change (PR #59 merged — `sql_jattr_combinator: "union"|"intersect"` on `SourceQuery`)

**Plan:** `/private/pinto-lab/plans/20261006_113117_ibd-fecal-metagenomic-with-calprotectin/plan.json`

## What changed vs. the initial run

The LLM now correctly emits `sql_jattr_combinator: "intersect"` for this question, with:
- native bucket: `librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND (biosamplemodel_sam ILIKE '%fecal%' OR '%gut%')`
- jattr: `["IBD diagnosis", "calprotectin measurement"]`, combinator `"all"`

Result: **436 rows** (down from 996,410 — the intersect mechanism works exactly as designed).

## But: zero overlap with gold (134 rows)

Checked directly: the native bucket alone *does* correctly match all 134 gold
(PRJNA1265906) samples (134/134, confirmed by query). The loss happens entirely on the
jattr side. The plan's own standalone per-concept counts show why:

- `"IBD diagnosis"` standalone: 97,987 biosamples matched
- `"calprotectin measurement"` standalone: 2,625 biosamples matched, across 10 distinct
  jattr keys: `calprotectin_sam`, `calprotectin_units_sam`, `calprotectin_numeric_sam`,
  `calprotectin_ave_sam`, `value_calprotectin_sam`, `Calprotectin`, `calprotectin`,
  `inhibitory calprotectin`, `subinhibitory calprotectin`, `calprotectin buffer`

**`fecalcalprotectin_run` — the exact key all 134 gold samples use — is not among the
matched keys.** The nearest-neighbor embedding lookup against `attribute_values_index`/
`attribute_keys_index` simply never surfaced it as similar enough to "calprotectin
measurement," despite ten other, more loosely-named calprotectin keys matching fine.

Since `jattr_combinator="all"` requires a sample to match BOTH concepts, and these 134
samples are entirely absent from the "calprotectin measurement" match set, they're
excluded regardless of what "IBD diagnosis" found for them.

## This is a separate, pre-existing bug, not a regression from the sql_jattr_combinator fix

Under the old (broken) union behavior this gap was invisible — the broad native-SQL
bucket swallowed everything into the result regardless of what jattr found, so a
concept-search miss never surfaced. The fix made the pipeline *correctly* depend on
jattr matching for this kind of question, which is exactly what exposed the miss.
**Fixing the union bug was necessary to even see this one.**

## Judgment: MISMATCH (recall 0% against this specific gold set)

The 436-row result is *precision-plausible* (10 real calprotectin jattr keys, properly
intersected with IBD-diagnosis concept matches) but misses the one bioproject
independently verified as ground truth entirely, due to the embedding gap above.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Two separate things to confirm:
1. Is the jattr concept-search embedding gap for `fecalcalprotectin_run` worth
   investigating/fixing (likely scope: LanceDB attribute_values_index re-embedding, or
   a key-name normalization step — concatenated `fecalcalprotectin_run` may embed
   poorly compared to the more conventional `calprotectin_*_sam` naming pattern)?
2. Is `jattr_combinator="all"` the right choice for "IBD diagnosis" + "calprotectin
   measurement" at all, given IBD context for a calprotectin study may be implicit at
   the study/publication level rather than recorded per-sample? If so, "any" (with a
   post-hoc researcher review of the union) might be the more honest default for this
   kind of compound clinical-context question — a separate, prompt-level decision from
   today's sql_jattr_combinator fix.
PARTIAL 2026-10-05T18:49:18 · initial · gold v1

# q01-ibd-calprotectin

**Question:** "IBD fecal metagenomic with calprotectin"

## Run: 2026-10-05_initial

**Plan:** `/private/pinto-lab/plans/20261005_184918_ibd-fecal-metagenomic-with-calprotectin/plan.json`

**Translation:** the translator correctly identified "IBD diagnosis" and "calprotectin measurement"
as `jattr_criteria` (combinator `"all"`), but *also* wrote a native-SQL bucket matching any
feces/gut-metagenome sample (`librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND (organism ILIKE '%feces
metagenome%' OR organism ILIKE '%human gut metagenome%' OR biosamplemodel 'human-gut')`), with
no disease/biomarker constraint at all.

**Why this produced 996,410 rows despite "correct" jattr concepts:** `CriterionResolver._resolve`
(`src/cohortquery/cohort.py`) combines the native-SQL bucket and the jattr-criteria bucket via set
**union**, by design (documented: "bucket 2's same-fact-recorded-twice case"). Union can only add
matches, never remove them. Since the native bucket alone already covers virtually every
feces/gut-metagenome sample in the mirror, the jattr narrowing became invisible in the final count
— confirmed: **no pipeline bug**, this is the correct, documented behavior; it's the translator's
bucket assignment that's wrong for this question. The native bucket should have stayed empty/narrow
(or been omitted, `sql=None`) and let `jattr_criteria` alone carry the IBD+calprotectin matching.

## Independent verification (live mirror queries, 2026-10-05)

- `bioproject = 'PRJNA1265906' AND json_extract_string(jattr, '$.fecalcalprotectin_run') IS NOT NULL`
  → **134 rows**
- `json_extract_string(jattr, '$.fecalcalprotectin_run') IS NOT NULL` (no bioproject filter, mirror-wide)
  → **134 rows** — identical count confirms this jattr key is exclusive to PRJNA1265906.
- Fecal calprotectin is a standard clinical biomarker used almost exclusively to monitor IBD
  (Crohn's disease / ulcerative colitis) disease activity — a dataset recording it is IBD research
  by biological/clinical convention, even though no literal "IBD" text appears anywhere in this
  bioproject's study/sample_name/attributes (confirmed by a prior session, 2026-08-25, recorded in
  `Scripts/eval/questions.jsonl`'s own history for a related, narrower-worded question).
- Set comparison: generated (996,410) ⊇ gold (134) exactly — **overlap = 134, gold-only (missed) = 0**.
  The translator's result is not wrong by omission; every correct answer is in there, buried among
  ~996,276 false positives.

## Judgment: PARTIAL

Recall = 100% (0 correct answers missed). Precision ≈ 0.0134% (134 / 996,410). Under the existing
`run_eval.py` scorer (exact set equality), this would score as a hard 0/MISMATCH — which is the
right call for that binary metric, but loses the nuance that the translator's jattr-concept
recognition itself worked; the failure is specifically in how the native-SQL bucket was scoped
alongside it.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

**What to confirm:** is `fecalcalprotectin_run` (134 rows, exclusively PRJNA1265906) the complete,
correct ground truth for "calprotectin measured," or should it also include adjacent jattr keys
seen in an older, pre-concept-search plan for a similarly-worded question
(`Workspaces/IBD_calprotectin/0_inputs/plan.json`, 1,261 rows via a broad OR of ~30 jattr key names
including `calprotectin_sam`, `calprotectin_numeric_sam`, `host_fecal_calprotectin_mgpergdryweight_sam`,
etc.) — those additional keys were NOT independently re-verified in this session (their real presence/
semantics on the current mirror is unconfirmed), so gold here is deliberately the narrow, fully-verified
134-row set rather than the broader, partially-unverified 1,261-row set. A researcher should say
whether the broader set's extra keys are genuine synonyms worth folding into gold v2.

## Why missed (translator)

Not a literal terminology gap — the translator *did* recognize the right concepts as jattr
criteria. The gap is architectural/prompt-level: the translator doesn't appear to understand that
the native-SQL bucket and jattr_criteria combine via union (additive), so writing a broad native
bucket "just in case" actively defeats the jattr narrowing instead of being a harmless redundant
check. Worth flagging to whoever owns `prompt.py`: the system prompt may need to state explicitly
that the native-SQL bucket is unioned in, not intersected, so an LLM's instinct to "also match
broadly for safety" needs to be suppressed for this field.

samples from bioproject PRJNA400072

# q02-prjna400072-bioproject-lookup

**Question:** "samples from bioproject PRJNA400072"

**Why this question:** mined from a historical pre-pivot `ask()` run
(`Workspaces/PRJNA400072/PRJNA400072/0_inputs/plan.json`). Deliberately the opposite case
from q01: a pure exact-match bioproject lookup with zero jattr ambiguity -- a control/baseline
contrast to q01's fuzzy-search-heavy case, exercising the simple native-SQL path end to end.

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Plan:** `/private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_115247_samples-from-bioproject-prjna400072/plan.json`

**Translation:**
```json
[
  {"source": "SRA_METADATA", "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE bioproject = 'PRJNA400072'"},
  {"source": "METALOG_METADATA", "sql": "SELECT biosample, sample_alias FROM metalog_metadata WHERE study_accession = 'PRJNA400072'"}
]
```

No jattr_criteria on either source -- correctly recognized as a pure native-column lookup.

## Independent verification (live mirror, 2026-10-19)

- `bioproject = 'PRJNA400072'` -> **220 biosamples**, matching the historical plan's own
  discovered count exactly.
- Checked whether the native `bioproject` column misses anything the historical plan's jattr
  fallback keys (`bioproject_accession_sam_s_dpl219`, `bioproject_id_sam`, etc.) would catch:
  **zero** extra biosamples found via fallback-only. The native column fully covers this
  bioproject; the historical plan's jattr hedge was unnecessary defensive breadth, not a real gap.
- Set comparison: generated (220) == gold (220) exactly. Overlap 220, missed 0, extra 0.

## Judgment: MATCH

Exact match, 100% recall and precision. Confirms the simple native-SQL exact-match path works
correctly end to end on the current pipeline (PR #59 + #61 + #62), independent of all the
jattr-search machinery q01 exercised.

human tumor samples from transcriptomic sequencing

q03-human-tumor-transcriptomic

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: fresh question authored to cover the "non-metagenome" gap (per project memory
--   project_askslm_not_metagenomics_only.md: STAT profiles every SRA run, so human-tumour
--   cohorts are valid science and must never be filtered out by a metagenome-only bias).
--   Independently verified: `is_tumor_sam` is a clean boolean-ish jattr key stored as a
--   single-element JSON array (["Yes"]/["No"], no other values, no mid-ground). Counting
--   DISTINCT biosamples (some biosamples carry multiple SRA runs, so a raw row count
--   overstates this): 33,405 distinct Homo sapiens / TRANSCRIPTOMIC biosamples have
--   is_tumor_sam[0] = 'Yes'.
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE organism = 'Homo sapiens'
  AND librarysource = 'TRANSCRIPTOMIC'
  AND json_extract_string(jattr, '$.is_tumor_sam[0]') = 'Yes';
PARTIAL 2026-10-19T12:30:49 · initial · gold v1

Correctly recognized as non-metagenomic (no metagenome bias). Overlap with narrow is_tumor_sam-based gold: 5212/33405 (15.6%). is_tumor_sam itself was never matched by any of the 3 jattr concepts used -- system instead matched ~211 other real tumor/cancer keys and values. Same class of finding as q01 (narrow single-key gold under-enumerates a content-matched population), not independently spot-checked further (time-boxed). See notes.md.

❓ Needs input: Is 15.6% overlap against this narrow gold acceptable given the q01 precedent, or does this concept area warrant value-aware re-scoring?

# q03-human-tumor-transcriptomic

**Question:** "human tumor samples from transcriptomic sequencing"

**Why this question:** fresh question authored to cover the "non-metagenome" benchmark gap
(per project memory: STAT profiles every SRA run, so human-tumour cohorts are valid science
and must never be filtered by a metagenome-only bias).

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Plan:** `/private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_123049_human-tumor-samples-from-transcriptomic/plan.json`

**Translation:**
```json
{
  "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE organism = 'Homo sapiens' AND (assay_type = 'RNA-Seq' OR librarysource IN ('TRANSCRIPTOMIC', 'METATRANSCRIPTOMIC'))",
  "jattr_criteria": ["tumor sample", "cancer sample", "neoplasm"],
  "jattr_combinator": "any",
  "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"
}
```

Correctly recognized as non-metagenomic (no librarysource=METAGENOMIC bias at all), correctly
intersected native RNA-Seq/transcriptomic scoping with tumor-concept jattr criteria.

## Verification against gold (distinct biosamples; see gold.sql)

- Gold: 33,405 (Homo sapiens / TRANSCRIPTOMIC / `is_tumor_sam[0]='Yes'`)
- Generated: 68,342
- Overlap: 5,212. Missed: 28,193. Extra: 63,130.

## Root cause: `is_tumor_sam` itself was never matched by any of the 3 jattr concepts

Checked `discovered.jattr_matches` directly: none of "tumor sample"/"cancer sample"/"neoplasm"
matched `is_tumor_sam` (confirmed: `'is_tumor_sam' in matched_on` is False for all three). The
system instead matched ~211 OTHER real tumor/cancer-related keys and values
(`tumor_sample_sam`, `cancer_sam`, `carcinoma_sam`, `neoplasia_type_sam`, free-text values like
`"Tumor"`/`"Neoplasm"`, etc.) -- a genuinely different, overlapping-but-distinct population from
my single-key gold definition. Same class of finding as q01: a narrow single-key gold
under-enumerates the real population a content-based search legitimately reaches via other
synonyms. Not independently spot-checked sample-by-sample here (time-boxed per Task 10's
breadth-over-depth goal) -- flagging as PARTIAL rather than fully re-deriving a value-aware
gold v2 the way q01 did.

## Judgment: PARTIAL

Low overlap against a narrow single-key gold, consistent with the already-understood pattern
(gold incompleteness for free-text/content-matched concepts) rather than a confirmed new defect.
Worth a deeper pass later if this concept area gets revisited.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Is 15.6% overlap (5212/33405) against this narrow gold acceptable given the q01 precedent
(gold incompleteness, not necessarily a system defect), or does this concept area warrant the
same value-aware re-scoring q01 got?

samples with recorded body mass index

q04-recorded-bmi-jattr-only

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: fresh question authored to cover the "jattr-only" gap (no native SQL column for
--   BMI exists anywhere in the mirror's schema -- this question can ONLY be answered via
--   jattr_criteria, sql=None is the only correct translation shape). Independently verified:
--   the two real, well-populated BMI-recording keys (bmi_sam, host_body_mass_index_sam_s_dpl230)
--   together cover 221,094 DISTINCT biosamples with a recorded BMI value (counting distinct
--   biosamples, not raw rows -- some biosamples carry multiple SRA runs).
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE jattr IS NOT NULL
  AND (json_extract_string(jattr, '$.bmi_sam') IS NOT NULL
       OR json_extract_string(jattr, '$.host_body_mass_index_sam_s_dpl230') IS NOT NULL);
PARTIAL 2026-10-19T12:32:27 · initial · gold v1

Correctly recognized sql=None for SRA_METADATA (no native BMI column) and correctly used Metalog native bmi column. Overlap 88458/221094 (40%). CONFIRMED BUG: host_body_mass_index_sam_s_dpl230 is entirely absent from attribute_keys_index -- excluded at build time by ID_NAME_RE bare substring match on "index" (matches body_mass_index same as it matches sample_index). Root-caused, not yet fixed. See notes.md.

plan: /private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_123227_samples-with-recorded-body-mass-index/plan.json

Generated SQL
-- SRA_METADATA: jattr-only, no native SQL

Notes
# q04-recorded-bmi-jattr-only

**Question:** "samples with recorded body mass index"

**Why this question:** fresh question authored to cover the "jattr-only" benchmark gap --
no native SQL column for BMI exists anywhere in the SRA metadata schema, so `sql=None` with
a pure jattr_criteria is the only correct translation shape for SRA_METADATA.

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Plan:** `/private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_123227_samples-with-recorded-body-mass-index/plan.json`

**Translation:**
```json
[
  {"source": "SRA_METADATA", "sql": null, "jattr_criteria": ["body mass index"], "jattr_combinator": "any", "sql_jattr_combinator": "union"},
  {"source": "METALOG_METADATA", "sql": "SELECT biosample, sample_alias FROM metalog_metadata WHERE bmi IS NOT NULL", "jattr_criteria": [], "jattr_combinator": "any", "sql_jattr_combinator": "union"}
]
```

Correctly recognized `sql=None` for SRA_METADATA (no native BMI column exists -- pure jattr
concept) and correctly used Metalog's own native `bmi` column where one DOES exist. Good
cross-source behavior: the same underlying concept resolved differently per source, each
correctly.

## Verification against gold (distinct biosamples; see gold.sql)

- Gold: 221,094 (`bmi_sam` OR `host_body_mass_index_sam_s_dpl230` non-null)
- Generated (SRA_METADATA only): 104,346
- Overlap: 88,458. Missed: 132,636. Extra: 15,888.

## Confirmed bug: `host_body_mass_index_sam_s_dpl230` is entirely absent from the rebuilt keys index

Checked `discovered.jattr_matches`: `bmi_sam` WAS matched (87,298 biosamples) but
`host_body_mass_index_sam_s_dpl230` was not among any matched key. Queried the real
`attribute_keys_index` directly for this exact key -- **zero rows**. It was excluded at
build time, not a retrieval-ranking miss (unlike q01's case).

**Root cause, confirmed:** `ID_NAME_RE` in `step7_build_attribute_indexes.py` includes a bare,
unanchored `index` alternative (meant to catch generic numeric index/position fields like
`sample_index`), but it matches as a **substring** anywhere in the key name -- so it also
silently excludes any biologically meaningful key that happens to contain "index" as part of
a compound word, like `body_mass_index`. Same severity class as the original
`fecalcalprotectin_run` incident (PR #61): a real, biologically important key silently dropped
regardless of its value. This is a build-time exclusion bug, not a retrieval-tuning question --
confirmed, reproducible, root-caused. **Not yet fixed** (deferred per 2026-10-19 decision: flag
now, continue authoring Task 10 questions, fix later as its own bug branch).

## Judgment: PARTIAL (confirmed defect, not yet fixed)

## needs_researcher_confirmation: no -- root cause is confirmed, not a judgment call.
Action needed: fix `ID_NAME_RE`'s `index` pattern. Note a word-boundary/token fix alone won't
separate the two cases cleanly -- `sample_index` and `body_mass_index` have the identical
`_index` suffix shape, so no regex can tell "generic row/position index" from "a named
biological index" by shape alone. The realistic fix is removing `index` from the denylist
entirely: presence-only matching is already value-agnostic and cheap, so a few harmless generic
index/position keys slipping into the search space costs far less than silently dropping a real
biological key like BMI. Then rebuild/patch the keys index and recheck this question.

healthy adult gut microbiome samples

q05-healthy-adult-gut-ambiguous

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: fresh question authored to cover the "ambiguous phrasing" gap. Deliberately NOT a
--   single gold query -- "healthy adult gut microbiome samples" is ambiguous on at least
--   three independent axes, and this question is scored by whether the translator's chosen
--   interpretation is DEFENSIBLE among the real plausible readings, not by exact-match
--   recall/precision against one picked answer (see notes.md for the scoring approach).
--
--   Axis 1 -- "healthy": could mean (a) an explicit control/healthy-labeled jattr value
--     (e.g. disease_state='control' or similar), (b) absence of any disease-type field
--     recorded at all, or (c) ignored entirely (no native column or clean jattr concept
--     cleanly means "healthy" in general, it's study-specific).
--   Axis 2 -- "adult": could mean (a) a specific age-type jattr field >= 18, (b) a
--     host_age_category/host_age_group value like "adult", or (c) ignored if no
--     age information is tractable for this question's scope.
--   Axis 3 -- "gut microbiome": could mean (a) librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND
--     organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' (the standard fecal-metagenome native-SQL pattern
--     used throughout this mirror, see q01), or (b) a broader jattr-based "gut" concept
--     search independent of librarysource.
--
-- No SELECT below -- there is no single correct answer to compare against by set overlap.
PARTIAL 2026-10-19T12:33:24 · initial · gold v1

No single gold -- scored by interpretation defensibility (see gold.sql for the 3 ambiguity axes). Metalog resolution (native environmental_package/age_category/subject_disease_status=CTR) is clean and fully defensible. SRA resolution folded three independent required facts (adult age, healthy status, no disease) into one flat jattr_criteria list with combinator=any, meaning a sample matching only ONE of the three counts -- looser than the question implies. Surfaces a real schema expressiveness gap (no way to require independent AND/OR sub-groups within one SourceQuery), not a resolver bug. See notes.md.

❓ Needs input: Is the flat jattr_combinator expressiveness gap worth a future design pass, or an intentional simplicity tradeoff?

plan: /private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_123324_healthy-adult-gut-microbiome-samples/plan.json

SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND (organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%human-gut%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%metagenome%');

-- METALOG_METADATA
SELECT biosample, sample_alias FROM metalog_metadata WHERE (environmental_package = 'human-gut' OR tissue_type = 'Stool') AND age_category IN ('adult', 'adolescent, adult') AND subject_disease_status = 'CTR';

Notes
# q05-healthy-adult-gut-ambiguous

**Question:** "healthy adult gut microbiome samples"

**Why this question:** fresh question authored to cover the "ambiguous phrasing" benchmark
gap. Deliberately vague on three axes (see gold.sql): what counts as "healthy", what age
threshold is "adult", what counts as "gut microbiome". Scored by whether the translator's
chosen interpretation is defensible, not by exact-match overlap against one picked answer.

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Plan:** `/private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_123324_healthy-adult-gut-microbiome-samples/plan.json`

**Translation (SRA_METADATA):**
```json
{
  "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' AND (organism ILIKE '%gut metagenome%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%human-gut%' OR array_to_string(biosamplemodel_sam, ' ') ILIKE '%metagenome%')",
  "jattr_criteria": ["adult age", "healthy status", "no disease"],
  "jattr_combinator": "any",
  "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"
}
```

**Translation (METALOG_METADATA):**
```sql
SELECT biosample, sample_alias FROM metalog_metadata
WHERE (environmental_package = 'human-gut' OR tissue_type = 'Stool')
  AND age_category IN ('adult', 'adolescent, adult')
  AND subject_disease_status = 'CTR'
```

Result: SRA_METADATA 255,474 biosamples; METALOG_METADATA 8,768.

## Interpretation chosen, per axis

- **Gut microbiome**: both sources used the standard metagenomic/fecal-sample native pattern
  (q01's own established pattern for SRA; Metalog's `environmental_package`/`tissue_type`).
  Defensible, the most common and least ambiguous reading.
- **Adult**: SRA folded "adult age" into the jattr_criteria list; Metalog used its native
  `age_category` column precisely (`'adult'` or `'adolescent, adult'`). Defensible on both sides.
- **Healthy**: SRA folded "healthy status" and "no disease" into the same jattr_criteria list;
  Metalog used `subject_disease_status = 'CTR'` (control) precisely. Metalog's choice is clean
  and defensible. SRA's is murkier -- see finding below.

## A real expressiveness gap, surfaced by this question

SRA_METADATA combined all three semantically distinct asks -- "adult age", "healthy status",
"no disease" -- into ONE flat `jattr_criteria` list with `jattr_combinator="any"`. That means a
biosample only needs to match **any one** of the three to count, not "adult AND (healthy OR
no-disease)" as the question actually implies. A sample matched purely via an age-type field,
with zero information about health/disease status, still counts as "healthy" under this
translation -- the "adult" and "healthy" requirements are independent, both-required facts, not
alternative phrasings of the same fact (contrast q01's calprotectin-key-synonym case, where
"any" correctly meant "any synonym of the same single fact").

This isn't a bug in the resolver (`jattr_combinator` behaves exactly as documented) -- it's a
genuine **expressiveness gap**: the current schema has one flat all-or-any combinator across an
entire jattr_criteria list, with no way to express "require axis A, and separately require (B or
C)". Flagging as an architecture observation, not something to fix now.

## Judgment: PARTIAL

Metalog's resolution is clean and fully defensible. SRA's resolution is defensible in its
native-SQL/gut-scoping half but structurally too loose in its age+health half, due to a real
schema expressiveness limit rather than a translator mistake per se.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Is this flat jattr_combinator expressiveness gap (can't require independent sub-conditions
within one SourceQuery) worth a future design pass, or is the current one-flat-list-per-source
shape an intentional simplicity tradeoff?

samples with antibiotic exposure

q06-antibiotic-exposure-jattr-only

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: fresh question authored for a second jattr-only concept (antibiotic exposure has no
--   native SQL column), chosen because it has a large real naming-variant neighborhood
--   (antibiotic_exposure_sam, antibiotics_run, antibiotic_treatment_sam, ... 300+ real
--   variants scouted earlier this session) -- a good breadth/precision test distinct from
--   q04's BMI case. Gold deliberately narrow: the two most literal, unambiguous keys.
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE jattr IS NOT NULL
  AND (json_extract_string(jattr, '$.antibiotic_exposure_sam') IS NOT NULL
       OR json_extract_string(jattr, '$.antibiotics_exposure_sam') IS NOT NULL);
PARTIAL 2026-10-19T13:02:17 · initial · gold v1

Correctly sql=None (no native column). Full recall against narrow 2-key gold (2874/2874), generated is a strict superset (75172 total). Same already-settled pattern as q01/q03: narrow gold undercounts a content-matched population. See notes.md.

-- reason: v1 conflated host SPECIES (Mus musculus, which includes every tissue/library type --
--   genomic, transcriptomic, single-cell, etc., most of which are NOT metagenomic gut samples)
--   with the actual metagenome-sample organism LABEL. The translator's own generated SQL used
--   `organism = 'mouse gut metagenome'` -- the standard NCBI organism-string convention for

--   "human gut metagenome" established in q01). This is MORE precise than v1, not less --
--   confirmed independently: 249,477 distinct biosamples, exactly matching the system's own
--   generated count. Corrected gold to the right, more precise definition rather than scoring
--   a correct answer as wrong.
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE organism = 'mouse gut metagenome'
  AND (librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' OR librarysource = 'METATRANSCRIPTOMIC');

# q07-mouse-gut-metagenome

**Question:** "mouse gut metagenome samples"

**Why this question:** extends the "non-metagenome bias" check to host diversity -- tests
that the pipeline doesn't implicitly assume "metagenome" means "human gut metagenome."

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Translation:**
```json
{"source": "SRA_METADATA",
 "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE organism = 'mouse gut metagenome' AND (librarysource = 'METAGENOMIC' OR librarysource = 'METATRANSCRIPTOMIC')",
 "jattr_criteria": []}
```
Pure native SQL, correctly used the exact NCBI organism-label convention ("mouse gut
metagenome", the same pattern as "human gut metagenome" from q01) rather than the broader
host-species name (Mus musculus, which spans all tissue/library types).

## Gold correction (v1 -> v2)

Initial gold used `organism = 'Mus musculus'` (45,806 biosamples) -- naively defined before
seeing the system's own translation. The system's more precise organism-label query (249,477
biosamples) is actually the *more correct* definition for "mouse gut metagenome samples", not
a looser one. Corrected gold to match (see gold.sql v2) rather than scoring a correct answer
as wrong.

## Verification against gold v2

- Gold: 249,477. Generated: 249,477. Overlap: 249,477. Missed: 0. Extra: 0.

## Judgment: MATCH

Exact match once gold reflects the right definition. Confirms non-metagenome-bias extends
correctly to non-human hosts, and that organism-label precision (not species name) is handled
correctly by the translator.

samples from patients over 65 years old

q08-age-over-65-numeric-threshold

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: capability-boundary question, NOT a set-overlap gold. "Over 65 years old" is a
--   numeric predicate on a value (age_sam / host_age_sam, both real, well-populated keys),
--   but JattrConceptIndex only does embedding-distance concept matching over key NAMES and
--   value CONTENT -- there is no mechanism anywhere in the translation/resolution pipeline
--   to express or evaluate a numeric threshold against a matched value. Expected outcome:
--   the translator either (a) ignores the numeric threshold and matches generically on an
--   age-presence concept (recall-favoring but imprecise -- includes all ages, not just >65),
--   or (b) attempts some native-SQL numeric cast against age_sam that may or may not work
--   given age_sam's real-world value messiness (free text, units, ranges all mixed in the
--   same key). This question is scored by documenting WHICH of these happened, not by a
--   set-overlap gold -- there is no reachable "gold" for "evaluate a numeric threshold" given
--   the current architecture has no such capability.
-- No SELECT below -- see notes.md for what was actually observed.

# q08-age-over-65-numeric-threshold

**Question:** "samples from patients over 65 years old"

**Why this question:** capability-boundary test -- does the pipeline have any way to
evaluate a numeric threshold against a jattr value?

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Translation:**
```json
[
  {"source": "SRA_METADATA", "sql": null, "jattr_criteria": ["age over 65 years old"]},
  {"source": "METALOG_METADATA", "sql": "SELECT biosample, sample_alias FROM metalog_metadata WHERE age_years > 65.0"}
]
```

**Metalog correctly used a real native numeric column** (`age_years > 65.0`) -- clean,
correct, exact threshold evaluation, because Metalog's harmonized schema has an actual numeric
age column.

**SRA has no native age column**, so it fell back to a single jattr_criteria phrase, "age over
65 years old", resolved via embedding-distance concept search (51 biosample matches). Inspected
the actual matched values directly: a MIX of correct (`>65`, `65 years old`, `65-year-old`) and
clearly wrong values (`age_above_45_sam` -- a 45 threshold, not 65; `60-65 years old` and
`60 - 65 years` -- ranges spanning BELOW 65; `over 60`, `older_60` -- a 60 threshold; `Age 64`
-- literally under 65). This directly confirms the predicted capability gap: the concept search
does pure text/embedding proximity to the literal digits "65", not actual numeric evaluation --
it cannot distinguish ">65" from "a nearby but different numeric threshold" or "a range that
happens to include 65."

## Judgment: capability gap confirmed (not a bug -- documents an architecture limit)

SRA-side results for this question are **not reliable** for a numeric-threshold question: some
matches are correct, some are off by the threshold itself. Metalog-side results are fully
correct because a real numeric column exists there. There is currently no mechanism anywhere
in JattrConceptIndex/CriterionResolver to evaluate `>`, `<`, or any numeric comparison against
a matched jattr value -- only embedding-distance "closeness in meaning," which happens to give
partial, unreliable signal for numeric concepts expressed as text.

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Worth a future design pass (e.g. a numeric-predicate criterion type, separate from
jattr_criteria's semantic matching) if numeric-threshold questions are expected to be common,
or is this an acceptable known limitation given Metalog already handles the clean cases?

samples collected in the United States

--   46,271 distinct biosamples via the two most literal diabetes keys.
--
-- SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
-- WHERE jattr IS NOT NULL
--   AND (json_extract_string(jattr, '$.diabetes_sam') IS NOT NULL
--        OR json_extract_string(jattr, '$.diabetes_type_sam_s_dpl165') IS NOT NULL);

-- version: 2 (current)
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: v1 missed that "patients" implies human -- diabetes_sam is overwhelmingly a MOUSE
--   disease-model field (45,736 total occurrences vs. only 9,221 when restricted to Homo
--   sapiens). The translator's own SQL correctly intersected with organism = 'Homo sapiens';
--   v1's naive gold, with no host filter, was wrong in the same direction as q07's mistake.
--   Corrected: 9,221 distinct Homo sapiens biosamples with diabetes_sam present.
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE organism = 'Homo sapiens'
  AND jattr IS NOT NULL
  AND (json_extract_string(jattr, '$.diabetes_sam') IS NOT NULL
       OR json_extract_string(jattr, '$.diabetes_type_sam_s_dpl165') IS NOT NULL);

# q12-type2-diabetes-jattr

**Question:** "samples from patients with type 2 diabetes"

**Why this question:** second disease concept (distinct from q03's tumor case) to broaden
disease-concept coverage.

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Translation:**
```json
{"source": "SRA_METADATA", "sql": "SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata WHERE organism = 'Homo sapiens'",
 "jattr_criteria": ["type 2 diabetes"], "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"}
```

## Gold correction (v1 -> v2), same pattern as q07

Initial gold had no host-organism filter: 46,271 biosamples. The translator's own SQL
intersected with `organism = 'Homo sapiens'` ("patients" implies human) -- checked directly:
`diabetes_sam` is overwhelmingly a MOUSE disease-model field in this mirror (45,736 total
occurrences, only 9,221 restricted to Homo sapiens). v1's naive gold was wrong in the same
direction as q07's mistake (conflating a jattr key's total reach with the human-only subset
the question actually implies). Corrected gold v2: 9,257 Homo sapiens biosamples.

## Verification against gold v2

- Gold: 9,257. Generated: 30,652. Overlap: 9,257 (100% recall). Extra: 21,395.

## Judgment: PARTIAL

Full recall against the corrected gold. Extra rows are the now-familiar, already-settled
pattern (real matches via other genuine diabetes-related keys/values my narrow 2-key gold
didn't enumerate -- consistent with q01/q06's precedent).

samples from infants

q14-infant-samples-young-age

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: fresh age-concept question at the opposite extreme from q08 ("over 65 years old")
--   -- infants. Unlike q08, there is no single "age_years" numeric column cleanly applicable
--   here (infant age is usually recorded in weeks/days, not years), so this tests whether the
--   jattr concept search handles a young-age concept as well as it (partially) handled an old-
--   age one. Independently verified: 32,039 distinct biosamples via the three most literal
--   infant-age keys (infant_age_sam, host_age_infant_sam, fed_as_infant_sam).
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE jattr IS NOT NULL
  AND (json_extract_string(jattr, '$.infant_age_sam') IS NOT NULL
       OR json_extract_string(jattr, '$.host_age_infant_sam') IS NOT NULL
       OR json_extract_string(jattr, '$.fed_as_infant_sam') IS NOT NULL);
PARTIAL 2026-10-19T14:08:32 · initial · gold v1

Correctly sql=None for SRA, Metalog used native age_category. Low overlap (1585/32039) against a narrow 3-key gold, but discovered.jattr_matches shows 57 real matches dominated by VALUE content (infant/Infant/human infant) not my hand-picked keys -- same already-established gold-incompleteness pattern as q01/q03, not independently re-derived (time-boxed).

plan: /private/pinto-lab/plans/20261019_140832_samples-from-infants/plan.json

# q14-infant-samples-young-age

**Question:** "samples from infants"

**Why this question:** age concept at the opposite extreme from q08 ("over 65 years old") --
tests whether the jattr concept search handles a young-age concept with the same (partial)
breadth it showed for the old-age one, without a clean numeric column to fall back on.

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Translation:**
```json
[
  {"source": "SRA_METADATA", "sql": null, "jattr_criteria": ["infant"]},
  {"source": "METALOG_METADATA", "sql": "SELECT biosample, sample_alias FROM metalog_metadata WHERE age_category IN ('baby', 'baby, child')"}
]
```
Correctly `sql=None` for SRA (no clean native infant-age column); Metalog used its own native
`age_category` column.

## Verification against narrow gold (infant_age_sam / host_age_infant_sam / fed_as_infant_sam)

- Gold: 32,039. Generated: 71,038. Overlap: 1,585. Missed: 30,454. Extra: 69,453.

Low overlap looks dramatic, but checked `discovered.jattr_matches` directly: the "infant"
concept matched 57 real keys/values, dominated by literal VALUES (`"infant"`, `"Infant"`,
`"infants"`, `"human infant"`) rather than my hand-picked 3-key list -- not shown surfacing
`infant_age_sam`/`host_age_infant_sam`/`fed_as_infant_sam` in the top 15 inspected. Same
already-established class of finding as q01/q03: a narrow single/few-key gold under-enumerates
a population reached mostly through VALUE content, not the specific keys I guessed. Not
independently re-derived with a broader gold (time-boxed, same reasoning as q03).

## Judgment: PARTIAL

Consistent with the q01/q03 precedent (gold incompleteness for value-matched concepts), not
confirmed as a system defect.

pregnant women samples

q15-pregnant-women-samples

Gold SQL
-- version: 1
-- date: 2026-10-19
-- reason: fresh health-state jattr concept with an unusually clean, literal, well-populated
--   single key (pregnant_sam) -- a good "should be easy" control case for the jattr-only
--   family, contrasting with q04/q06/q12/q13's messier multi-key neighborhoods.
--   Independently verified: 35,544 distinct biosamples.
SELECT biosample FROM sra_metadata
WHERE jattr IS NOT NULL AND json_extract_string(jattr, '$.pregnant_sam') IS NOT NULL;
PARTIAL 2026-10-19T14:09:13 · initial · gold v1

Translator required jattr_combinator=all across ["pregnant status", "female sex"] -- but female sex is redundant (implied by pregnancy), not independent. Confirmed: pregnant_sam alone matches 35544, but intersecting with a separately-recorded sex field wrongly excludes samples lacking that redundant field. Overlap 17773/35544 (50% recall). Mirror image of q05s finding (there, independent facts wrongly collapsed into any; here, a redundant fact wrongly required via all).

❓ Needs input: Same open question as q05: worth a translator-prompt guideline, or acceptable occasional miscalibration?

# TRUNCATED: showing 200 of 32003 rows. Full result count recorded in index.csv's result_row_count column.
Notes
# q15-pregnant-women-samples

**Question:** "pregnant women samples"

**Why this question:** clean, literal, well-populated single key (`pregnant_sam`) chosen as a
"should be easy" control case -- instead surfaced a genuine combinator finding.

## Run: 2026-10-19_initial

**Translation:**
```json
{"source": "SRA_METADATA", "sql": null,
 "jattr_criteria": ["pregnant status", "female sex"],
 "jattr_combinator": "all", "sql_jattr_combinator": "intersect"}
```

## Verification against gold (`pregnant_sam` non-null)

- Gold: 35,544. Generated: 31,711. Overlap: 17,773. Missed: 17,771. Extra: 13,938.

## Finding: "all" combinator over a redundant fact, the mirror image of q05

The translator required BOTH "pregnant status" AND "female sex" to match on the same sample
(`jattr_combinator="all"`). But "female sex" is not an independent fact here -- it's implied by
pregnancy, not a separate requirement the question actually asks for. Confirmed directly:
`pregnant_sam` alone matches 35,544 biosamples, but many of those don't ALSO carry a
separately-recorded sex-type field, so the "all" intersection with "female sex" wrongly
excludes genuinely pregnant samples. This is the inverse of q05's finding: there, independent
facts (adult AND healthy) were wrongly collapsed into "any" (too loose); here, a single fact
with a redundant restatement was wrongly required via "all" (too strict). Both point at the
same root cause: a flat `jattr_combinator` per source can't distinguish "these are alternate
phrasings of one fact" from "these are genuinely separate required facts" -- that judgment is
made per-question by the translator, and it got this one backwards.

## Judgment: PARTIAL

## needs_researcher_confirmation: yes

Same open question as q05: is this flat-combinator expressiveness/judgment issue worth a
translator-prompt-level guideline (e.g. don't require a redundant demographic fact alongside
a concept that already implies it), or an acceptable, occasional miscalibration?
